\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{h_{k}})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{h_{k}}
.
Fix
2\leq i\leq k
,
h_{i-1}+1\leq l\leq h_{i}
, and let
\mathcal{Q}_{l}
denote the
(l-h_{i-1})
-th entry of

\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}
.
Since
\Delta_{\lambda}\colon\mathbb{G}\to\mathbb{G}
is a group homomorphism,
Eq.

A.9

implies
\mathcal{Q}_{l}(\Delta_{\lambda}x,\Delta_{\lambda}y)=\lambda^{i}\mathcal{Q}_{l}(x,y)
,
and therefore (see
[
BLU07
, Proposition 1.3.4]
)

\mathcal{Q}_{l}(x,y)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma,\theta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{h_{k}},\\
|\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}}+|\theta|_{\mathbb{G}}=i\end{subarray}}q_{l}(\sigma,\theta)x^{\sigma}y^{\theta},

for some
q_{l}(\sigma,\theta)\in\mathbb{R}
.
By definition of
|\cdot|_{\mathbb{G}}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{l}(x,0)=\mathcal{Q}_{l}(0,x)=0
(which follows from
0
being the identity element of
\mathbb{G}
),
we deduce
q_{l}(\sigma,\theta)=0
if
\sigma=0
or
\theta=0
or
\sigma_{t}+\theta_{t}>0
for some
t>h_{i-1}
.
Thus,
q_{l}(\sigma,\theta)\neq 0
implies

\displaystyle|x^{\sigma}|

\displaystyle=|x_{1}^{\sigma_{1}}\cdots x_{h_{i-1}}^{\sigma_{h_{i-1}}}|\leq\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\prod_{t=h_{j-1}+1}^{h_{j}}|x_{t}|^{\frac{j\sigma_{t}}{j}}\lesssim\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\prod_{t=h_{j-1}+1}^{h_{j}}\|x^{(j)}\|_{j}^{\frac{j\sigma_{t}}{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\sum_{t=h_{j-1}+1}^{h_{j}}j\,\sigma_{t}}=\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{|\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}}}

and similarly
|y^{\theta}|\lesssim\|y\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{|\theta|_{\mathbb{G}}}
.
The thesis then follows from

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}_{l}(x,y)|\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma,\theta\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{h_{k}}\setminus\{0\},\\
|\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}}+|\theta|_{\mathbb{G}}=i\end{subarray}}\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{|\sigma|_{\mathbb{G}}}\|y\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{|\theta|_{\mathbb{G}}}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{j}\|y\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{i-j}\lesssim\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{j}\|y\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{i-j}.

∎

Proof of
A.7
.

The thesis is equivalent to the existence of a homogeneous norm
N
on
\mathbb{G}

satisfying

N(x\cdot y)\leq N(x\cdot z)+N(w\cdot y)

for
x,y,z,w\in\mathbb{G}
with
z^{(1)}=-w^{(1)}
and
N(z),N(w)\leq\delta<1\leq N(x),N(y)
.
Fix norms
\|\cdot\|_{1},\dots,\|\cdot\|_{k}
on
\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}},\dots,\mathbb{R}^{m_{k}}
respectively.
For
\omega=(\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{k})\in(0,\infty)^{k}
and
1\leq i\leq k
,
set

(A.11)

N_{\omega,i}(x):=\max_{1\leq j\leq i}\omega_{j}\|x^{(j)}\|_{j}^{\frac{1}{j}},\qquad N_{\omega}(x):=N_{\omega,k}(x),

for
x=(x^{(1)},\dots,x^{(k)})\in\mathbb{G}
.
It is clear that
N_{\omega}
satisfies all conditions in
Eq.

A.6
, except possibly for
triangle inequality.
The proof of
[
FSSC03
, Theorem 5.1]
shows
that for any
1<i\leq k
if
N_{\omega,i-1}
satisfies triangle inequality,
then so does
N_{\omega,i}
, provided
\omega_{i}
is chosen small enough w.r.t.
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{i-1}>0
.
We claim that for
1\leq i\leq k
there are
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{i}>0
such that
N_{\omega,i}

satisfies triangle inequality and, if so does
N_{\omega}
, then

(A.12)

N_{\omega,i}(x\cdot y)\leq N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)+N_{\omega}(w\cdot y),

for
x,y,z,w
as in
Eq.

A.11
with
N=N_{\omega}
.
The thesis will then follow taking
i=k
and setting
N:=N_{\omega}
.

We proceed by induction on
1\leq i\leq k
.
If
i=1
, then triangle inequality and
Eq.

A.12
hold for any
\omega_{1}>0
.
Suppose now
i>1
and let
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{i-1}>0
be such that the claim holds with
i\equiv i-1
.
Let
\omega_{i}>0
be small (to be determined depending on
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{i-1}
),
suppose
N_{\omega}
satisfies triangle inequality, and let

x,y,z,w\in\mathbb{G}
be as in
Eq.

A.11
with
N=N_{\omega}
.
Since
\|\cdot\|_{i}
is a norm on
\mathbb{R}^{m_{i}}
, we have

(A.13)

\displaystyle\|x^{(i)}+y^{(i)}+\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,y)\|_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\|x^{(i)}+z^{(i)}+\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,z)\|_{i}+\|w^{(i)}+y^{(i)}+\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(w,y)\|_{i}

\displaystyle+\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,y)\|_{i}+\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,z)\|_{i}+\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(w,y)\|_{i}

\displaystyle+\|z^{(i)}\|_{i}+\|w^{(i)}\|_{i}.

The assumptions on
x,y,z,w
and
N_{\omega}
imply

1-\delta\leq(1-\delta)N_{\omega}(x)\leq N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)\leq(1+\delta)N_{\omega}(x)

and similarly for
N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)
;
in particular,

(A.14)

\max(N_{\omega}(z),N_{\omega}(w))\leq\delta\leq\frac{\delta}{1-\delta}\min(N_{\omega}(x\cdot z),N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)).

We then deduce from
Lemma

A.10

(A.15)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,y)\|_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\|x\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{j}\|y\|_{\mathbb{G},i-1}^{i-j}

\displaystyle\leq\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{-i}\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega,i-1}(x)^{j}N_{\omega,i-1}(y)^{i-j}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{-i}\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(x,z)\|_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{-i}\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j},

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(w,y)\|_{i}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{-i}\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j}.

Eq.

A.14
also implies

\displaystyle\|z^{(i)}\|_{i}+\|w^{(i)}\|_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{i}^{-i}(N_{\omega}(z)^{i}+N_{\omega}(w)^{i})\leq\frac{2\delta^{i}}{\omega_{i}^{i}(1-\delta)^{i}}\min(N_{\omega}(x\cdot z),N_{\omega}(w\cdot y))^{i}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\delta^{i}}{\omega_{i}^{i}(1-\delta)^{i}}\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j}

which, together with

Eqs.

A.13
,
A.15
and
A.9
, gives

(A.16)

\displaystyle\omega_{i}^{i}\|(x\cdot y)^{(i)}\|_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{i}^{i}\|(x\cdot z)^{(i)}\|_{i}+\omega_{i}^{i}\|(w\cdot y)^{(i)}\|_{i}

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{C\omega_{i}^{i}}{\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{i}}+\frac{2\delta^{i}}{(1-\delta)^{i}}\right]\max_{1\leq j\leq i-1}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j},

for some constant
C>0
independent of
\omega
.
Since
\delta\in(0,1/2)
, it holds
\delta(1-\delta)^{-1}<1

and, if
\omega_{i}>0
was chosen small enough w.r.t.
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{i-1}
, we have

\frac{C\omega_{i}^{i}}{\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}\omega_{j}^{i}}+\frac{2\delta^{i}}{(1-\delta)^{i}}\leq 2;

by (the proof of)
[
FSSC03
, Theorem 5.1]
8
8

8

In fact, the proof of
[
FSSC03
, Theorem 5.1]

consists of the
the same argument exhibited here, but with
z=w=0
and triangle inequality of
N_{\omega,i}
as induction hypothesis.

,
we may
take
\omega_{i}>0
so small that, in addition,
N_{\omega,i}
satisfies triangle inequality.
Since
\min_{1\leq j\leq i-1}{i\choose j}={i\choose\lfloor i/2\rfloor}\geq 2^{\lfloor i/2\rfloor}\geq 2
,

Eq.

A.16
yields

\omega_{i}^{i}\|(x\cdot y)^{(i)}\|_{i}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{i}{i\choose j}N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)^{j}N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)^{i-j}=\big(N_{\omega}(x\cdot z)+N_{\omega}(w\cdot y)\big)^{i}.

Hence, the induction hypothesis and the definition of
N_{\omega,i}
imply that
Eq.

A.12
holds,
concluding the proof of the claim.
∎

Remark A.17
.

We do not know what is the optimal value of
\delta
in
A.7
;
nonetheless, one cannot do better than
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
.
To see this, let
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{k}}
be a Carnot group of step
k\geq 2
,

d
an invariant distance on
\mathbb{G}
,
\delta>0
, and suppose they satisfy the conclusion of
A.7
.
Let
m_{1}+1\leq l\leq m_{1}+m_{2}
,

u=(0,\dots,0,u_{l},0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{G}
with
d(u,0)=1
, and set

x:=y^{-1}:=u
,
z:=w^{-1}:=\Delta_{\delta}u
,
so that
d(x,0)=d(y,0)=1
and
d(z,0)=d(w,0)=\delta
.
Observe that

\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(u,u)=\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(-\Delta_{\delta}u,u)=\mathcal{Q}^{(i)}(u,-\Delta_{\delta}u)=0
for
1\leq i\leq k
;
see
[
BLU07
, Proposition 2.2.22(4)]
.
It follows that
y^{-1}\cdot x=\Delta_{\sqrt{2}}u
,
z^{-1}\cdot x=y^{-1}\cdot w=\Delta_{\sqrt{1-\delta^{2}}}u

and therefore

\sqrt{2}=d(x,y)\leq d(x,z)+d(w,y)=2\sqrt{1-\delta^{2}},

which implies
\delta\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}
.

Consider a Carnot group
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{k}}
of step
k
,
let
s=\sum_{i=1}^{k}i\,m_{i}

denote its
homogeneous dimension
,
and let
d
be an invariant distance on
\mathbb{G}
.
Then there is a constant
c(d)>0
such that

\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d}(B_{d}(x,r))=c(d)r^{s}

x\in\mathbb{G}
, and
0<r<\infty
; see e.g.
[
BLU07
, Remark 1.4.5]
(and
2.42
).
In particular, if
d
is chosen as in
A.7
,
then
(\mathbb{G},d,\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d})
is an
s
-ADR metric measure space with shortcuts (by
Lemma

A.2
).
Since it is also a
1
-PI space (see e.g.
[
SC16
, Theorem 3.25]
),
by
[
CK09
]
we deduce that it is a
Y
-LDS for any Banach space
Y
with RNP.
From Pansu’s differentiation theorem with
\mathbb{R}
as target (see
[
Pan89
]
or
[
Don24
, Theorem 10.3.2]
),
we see that
(\mathbb{G},d,\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d})
is an LDS with single chart
(\mathbb{G},\varphi\colon\mathbb{G}\to\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}})
,

\varphi(x)=x^{(1)}
, for
x=(x^{(1)},\dots,x^{(k)})\in\mathbb{G}
.
In particular, the Cheeger differentiable structure is compatible with the shortcuts.

Hence, we have the following.

Corollary A.18
.

Let
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{k}}
be a Carnot group of step
k\geq 2
,
homogeneous dimension
s
, and
let
d
be an invariant distance as in
A.7
.

Then
(\mathbb{G},d,\mathcal{H}_{d}^{s})
is an
s
-ADR
1
-PI space with compatible shortcuts.
Hence, for
\eta=(\eta_{i})\subseteq(0,1]
, the following hold:

•

(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta},\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d})
is PI rectifiable if there is
I\subseteq\mathbb{N}
such that

\sum_{i\in I}\eta_{i}^{s}<\infty
and
\inf\{\eta_{i}\colon i\notin I\}>0
,
and purely PI unrectifiable otherwise;

•

for any non-zero Banach space
Y
with RNP,
(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta},\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d})
is a
Y
-LDS
if and only if
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d}(\operatorname{Bad}(f))=0
for every Lipschitz map
f\colon(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta})\to Y
;

•

for any Banach space
Y
with RNP and Lipschitz map
f\colon(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta})\to Y
,
no positive-measure
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d}
-measurable subset of
\operatorname{Bad}(f)
is a
Y
-LDS.

Proof.

The first item follows from
4.3
and
4.1
,
while the second and third from (
Lemma

2.11
and)
5.7
and
5.8
, respectively.
∎

We do not know if
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d}(\mathrm{Bad}(f))=0
for
Lipschitz
f\colon(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta})\to\mathbb{R}

(let alone for
f\colon(\mathbb{G},d_{\eta})\to Y
and
Y\neq 0
Banach space with RNP).
This question is related to the ‘Vertical vs Horizontal’ Poincaré inequalities in Carnot groups,
see
[
ANT13
,
Ryo25
]
,
but does not seem to be directly implied by such results.

Nonetheless, we have the following application.

Corollary A.19
.

Let
\mathbb{G}=\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{R}^{m_{k}}
be a Carnot group of step
k\geq 2
,

d_{\mathbb{G}}
an invariant distance on
\mathbb{G}
,
and let
s
denote the
homogeneous dimension of
\mathbb{G}
.

Then there is a complete
s
-ADR purely PI unrectifiable metric measure space
(X,d_{X},\mathcal{H}^{s}_{X})
and a David-Semmes regular
homeomorphism
f\colon(\mathbb{G},d_{\mathbb{G}})\to(X,d_{X})
.
In particular,
f
is biLipschitz on no positive-measure
\mathcal{H}^{s}_{d_{\mathbb{G}}}
-measurable set.

Proof.

Follows from
A.18
,
Lemmas

3.14
,
3.13
and
3.18
,
and the fact that
\mathbb{G}
is a PI space (or by
3.32
).
∎

A.19
provides a complete negative answer to
[
Sem01
, Conjecture 5.2]

and one of its variants
[
Sem01
, Section 5.2]
.
When
\mathbb{G}
is the first Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}^{1}
, this is due to
[
LDLR17
]
.
In the recent
[
LS25
]
, the authors provide a close-to-optimal
answer to the question of when Lipschitz (or DS-regular) maps have biLipschitz pieces.
In particular,
[
LS25
]
also resolves the above questions of Semmes.
